*   As a subsequent result of the previous point, AI also increases productivity and efficiency in various processes, allowing organizations to focus on more strategic and value-added activities.
*   AI helps in saving labor and gets done heavy labor-oriented tasks.
*   Of course, in any job - data or details heavy or otherwise - AI reduces the time spent on the work. This is true for all sorts of industries - from finance to security and fraud detection to healthcare to IT.
*   AI-based systems are active 24/7, so they are good for jobs in customer service and other sectors that require constant assistance.
*   AI-powered systems are great for the customization of content. They can personalize user experiences based on individual preferences and behavior. From personalized product recommendations in e-commerce to personalized content suggestions on social media, AI enhances user satisfaction and engagement.
*   AI systems deliver output consistent in quality regardless of the job they are deployed in.
*   AI excels at efficient forecasting by predicting future trends based on historical data patterns, which is particularly useful in industries like finance, healthcare, and marketing, where predictive analytics can optimize strategies and outcomes.
*   AI models can be made to continuously learn from new data so they adapt to changing circumstances and improve their performance over time. This makes AI systems more accurate and effective as they continue to gain more experience forever.
*   AI can work to optimize resource allocation in sectors like energy management, logistics, and transportation, leading to cost savings and efficient resource utilization.

[Disadvantages of AI](#contents.xhtml#sc4_45a)

The following are some of the disadvantages of AI:

*   Training AI models can be expensive and energy-intensive due to their high compute requirements.
*   Creating AI systems requires a high degree of technical skills, including programming and data analysis. Successful AI development also needs access to essential technical resources like advanced hardware and software.
*   AI systems can inherit biases from the data used in training, especially at high scalability requirements. This can result in unfair outcomes, discrimination, and perpetuation of societal biases if not carefully managed.
*   Further, the accuracy of any AI model depends mostly on the quality and diversity in the data used to train the model. Therefore, any biased, wrong, or incomplete data can make for inaccurate outputs.
*   AI systems often require access to vast amounts of data for training, which raises many valid concerns about privacy and data security. Anything but careful handling of data can lead to security breaches and compromise user privacy.
*   There are ethical concerns involved in AI, too: the elimination of human jobs brought on by AI can increase rates of unemployment, making for an imbalanced societal structure.
*   While AI systems may lack full human-like creativity and emotional intelligence, they do exhibit certain creative capabilities. The extent of their creativity remains a topic of debate, yet LLMs are increasingly utilized in creative fields such as media, entertainment, and marketing.
*   Overreliance on AI systems without human supervision can bring catastrophic consequences, especially if the AI makes any erroneous predictions.

[Table 2.1](#ch02.xhtml#tab2-1) shows a quick glance at the most prominent advantages and disadvantages of AI:

| Advantages of AI || Disadvantages of AI |
| --- | --- |
| 

*   AI systems save up on human effort and time.
*   They are less susceptible to human errors.
*   AI-powered systems automate labor-intensive tasks.
*   AI-based systems are active at all times, which makes them particularly suitable to areas like customer service.
*   AI-powered systems are incredible for customization of content, enhancing user experience.
*   AI can predict future outcomes and trends based on historical data patterns.
*   AI models can be made to continuously learn from new data, boosting their accuracy.
*   AI can make for efficient resource utilization in various sectors.

 || 

*   Training AI models can be expensive and energy-intensive.
*   Creating AI systems requires highly technically knowledgeable individuals.
*   AI systems can inherit biases from the data used in training.
*   Biased, wrong, or incomplete data fed to AI-powered systems can make for inaccurate outputs.
*   AI systems require vast amounts of data for training, raising concerns about privacy and data security.
*   Initially, AI eliminates many human jobs, increasing unemployment rates.
*   AI systems lack full human-like creativity and emotional intelligence but show some creative abilities and are increasingly used in creative fields.

 |

Table 2.1: Advantages vs. disadvantages of AI

As we know, technologies like AI are not inherently good or bad; they are simply tools in the hands of humans to use as we see fit. So, most of the aforementioned disadvantages of AI can be solved with human consideration. It is up to us to remember the ethical side and put human requirements first.

Further, addressing issues of privacy and security may call for a multidisciplinary approach involving policymakers, technologists, ethicists, and the overall society as a whole. It falls upon all of us to make sure that AI systems are being developed and used responsibly, with transparency, and in an overall way that, again, puts human needs and morals first.

[Categories of AI](#contents.xhtml#sc3_46a)

AI can be categorized into strong and weak AI, which are discussed as follows:

*   A strong AI system, also called artificial general intelligence (AGI), comprises programming that is powerful enough to replicate the cognitive abilities that a human brain possesses. So, if this kind of AI encounters a task unfamiliar to it, it is strong enough to apply its knowledge from its specific domain to the new one presented to it and come up with a solution. Such an AI would have a tremendous advantage over human intelligence because while it would be human-level intelligence, it would perform tasks billions of times faster thanks to its semiconductor hardware.
*   However, weak AI or narrow AI can complete only the specific kind of task it is trained to tackle. Virtual personal assistants like Alexa are good examples of such weak AI.

According to Arend Hintze, an assistant professor of integrative biology and computer science and engineering at Michigan State University, AI can further be categorized into these four categories:

*   Reactive AI systems, which have no memory and are trained in relation to a specific task. It can make predictions, but due to the lack of a memory, cannot use past experiences to apply gained knowledge to future ones. 
*   AI systems with limited memory. These can use past experiences to come up with informed results in future tasks. The decision-making part in self-driving cars may be created this way.
*   Borrowing from the psychological term theory of mind, AI systems can be made to have an adequate social intelligence to comprehend emotions. This kind of AI may be able to understand human intentions and predict human behavior, so they can act as members in teams that comprise both human and AI systems. The system may not truly understand emotions. It would simply be capable of reacting to them in a way that seems human.
*   Self-aware AI systems, which would have a sense of self and possess consciousness. These AI systems would be able to understand and form a state of mind so as to speak. Of course, this type does not exist yet.

[Applications of AI](#contents.xhtml#sc3_47a)

AI can be used in a wide variety of industries to improve processes, decision-making, and customer experiences. Some of the industries where AI can be applied include:

*   Healthcare: AI is already being used in medical imaging for accurate diagnosis or in drug discovery. It can find applications in personalized treatment plans, remote monitoring for patients, and even virtual health assistants.
*   Education: AI can be applied to bring adaptive learning platforms, intelligent tutoring systems that will help students learn at their own pace, and automated grading systems.
*   E-commerce: AI can be used in this field for product recommendations, understanding and forecasting customer demand, inventory management, and of course, chatbots for customer support.
*   Finance: AI-based systems can be used here to process large amounts of data, fraud detection, algorithmic trading, personalized financial advice, and credit risk assessment, to name a few uses.
*   Optimization in transportation and logistics: AI is used here for route optimization, predictive maintenance, forecasting, and even autonomous vehicles, once that goes mainstream.
*   Optimization in manufacturing: Yet another sector where AI can help with optimization and streamlining of all processes, aid with predictive maintenance, supervise control, and automate various steps.
*   Marketing and advertising: AI-powered systems can be used for targeted advertising and content personalization to enhance user experience, and further be employed in sentiment analysis and customer segmentation.
*   Agriculture: AI here can be used for monitoring crops and automating farming equipment.
*   Real estate: In this sector, AI can be used for unbiased property valuation, personalized recommendations in real estate, and, obviously, virtual tours of properties.

Of course, these are only a few examples of the vast number of industries where AI can bring significant changes. AI can also be combined with other emerging technologies to bring in products and services never seen before.

Let us now discuss blockchain.

[Understanding blockchain](#contents.xhtml#sc2_48a)

Since we know the basics of blockchain already, this is our opportunity to explore it a little more in-depth. First, let us understand the architecture of a blockchain.

[Underlying architecture of a blockchain](#contents.xhtml#sc3_49a)

The following figure showcases the architecture of blockchain technology:

![](images/Figure_2.1.png)

Figure 2.1: Architecture of Blockchain technology  
Source: [https://www.mdpi.com/2071-1050/14/13/7633](https://www.mdpi.com/2071-1050/14/13/7633))

The architecture of a blockchain is the design and structure of the decentralized ledger protocol that enables the recording and verification of transactions and is spread out through a global network of nodes. 

So, what does your average blockchain consist of in terms of the architecture underneath? Here are the essential components:

*   Distributed ledger: This is the fundamental building block of a blockchain. The records of blockchain are spread across a worldwide network of nodes, and thus the chain of information is kept tamper-proof and transparent.
*   Consensus mechanism: As we know, this is how a blockchain achieves agreement among all its nodes and maintains decentralization. Ever since Ethereum initiated its transition to a proof-of-stake (PoS) consensus protocol, the concept has generated a lot of attention. With the PoS consensus mechanism in particular, nodes usually need to deposit a certain amount of the native token to qualify as a potential validator of transactions on the chain. Once they successfully do their job and update the blockchain, they are rewarded with a part of the transaction fees the platform acquires from users. 
*   Cryptographic hashing: Blockchains rely on cryptographic hashing to ensure the integrity of data recorded on the chains, as well as fortify security. Each of the blocks on the chain therefore contains a unique cryptographic hash that is calculated based upon the data within the block, as well as the hash of the previous block on the chain. Any change in the data contained in any of the blocks on the chain would cause its hash to change, causing discrepancies in all subsequent blocks as well, making tampering easily detectable.
*   Smart contracts: These are self-executing pieces of code stored on the blockchain which are programmed with predefined rules and conditions. Smart contracts execute themselves when the specified conditions are met. They allow for lower human intervention and automated processes.
*   Merkle trees: These are data structures that are used to bring efficiency to the process of verifying large sets of data. A tree structure is formed by hashing individual elements of data, and then hashing the outputs in pairs until one ultimate output is reached. Merkle Tree structures work to enable faster and more scalable verification of large sets of data in a block.
*   Public/private key pairs: These are cryptographic tools to maintain privacy and verifiability for blockchain transactions. Users are provided these keys uniquely to every person, the public key to receive transactions from others, and the private key to sign and initiate transactions, and prove ownership over funds.
*   Network protocols: These make communication between nodes on a blockchain possible. They lay down rules for how nodes communicate, help synchronize data and reach consensus of the current state of the blockchain.

Now, these were all the fundamental components in the architecture of an average blockchain that you may see. Do remember that different blockchain networks may have variations in their architecture, based upon the specific use case and design goals associated with them.

[Advantages vs. disadvantages of blockchain](#contents.xhtml#sc3_50a)

Now, just like any other technological development, blockchain has its own set of advantages and disadvantages. Let us check these out.

[Advantages of blockchain](#contents.xhtml#sc4_51a)

Blockchain offers a whole plethora of advantages that make it a much-needed technology for a wide range of applications. Some key advantages of blockchain include:

*   Blockchain brings much-needed decentralization to any organizational structure you may think of. This enhances security, privacy, and transparency, as there is no centralized point of control.
*   Once data is recorded on the blockchain, it is essentially immutable due to the cryptographic hash. No bad actor can alter the sequence of blocks or data held in them without notifying the entire network.
*   Advanced cryptographic techniques are employed on blockchains to lend security to all data and transactions. Further, the distributed nature of the network and consensus mechanisms all work to make blockchains resistant to hacking and unauthorized alterations.
*   Blockchain allows for trustless transactions, so all counterparties can engage in transactions without requiring a trusted intermediary. Smart contracts instead come in to automatically execute said exchange.
*   Due to the transparent and immutable nature of blockchain, participants can easily trace the origin and movement of transactions, again ensuring authenticity and reducing fraud.
*   Blockchains can enable faster and more streamlined transactions when it comes to cross-border payments. As a consequence of the reduction of intermediaries and faster processes, blockchains also reduce transaction costs.
*   Blockchain's open-source nature and permissionless platforms encourage innovation upon existing developments, as well as facilitate the creation of new applications and solutions across industries as per consumer and industry demands.

[Disadvantages of blockchain](#contents.xhtml#sc4_52a)

While blockchain offers many, many advantages, it also faces challenges. Let us look at some of these roadblocks:

*   As one of the most prevalent issues for blockchains currently, these networks face scalability problems when handling a large number of transactions. So, as the number of participants and transactions increase on a blockchain, it can slow down and result in longer confirmation times and higher transaction fees.
*   Proof-of-work blockchains like Bitcoin can be energy-intensive, leading to environmental concerns.
*   As each full node on the blockchain has to store a copy of the entire ledger, this can make for expansive data storage needs. This is especially true for public blockchains with sizable transaction histories.
*   Interoperability is another challenge blockchains still face, so most modern ones still lack seamless interoperability.
*   Blockchains can fall vulnerable to risks like private key theft, smart contract vulnerabilities, and 51% attacks on proof-of-work networks.
*   As we know, transactions of blockchains are irreversible, which can make it hard to rectify and salvage the situation in case a fraud or any error happens.

[Table 2.2](#ch02.xhtml#tab2-2) shows the advantages and disadvantages of blockchain:

| Advantages of blockchain || Disadvantages of blockchain |
| --- | --- |
| 

*   Decentralization, so no central authority is watching over your funds or transitions.
*   Enhanced security, privacy, and transparency due to decentralization.
*   Immutability of data.
*   Cryptographic hashing to secure data.
*   Trustless transactions.
*   Transparency and traceability.
*   Faster transactions, especially for cross-border applications.
*   Reduced transaction fees for the lack of intermediaries and automated processes.
*   Open-source coding encourages further development with ease.

 || 

*   Scalability issues once the network grows in size.
*   Energy intensiveness due to the consensus mechanisms like proof-of-work.
*   Storage concerns for public blockchains as they grow.
*   Lack of interoperability between different blockchain networks.
*   Vulnerability to security threats like the 51% attack or smart contract malfunctions.
*   Irreversibility of transactions makes errors/fraudulent activities hard to turn back.

 |

Table 2.2: Advantages and disadvantages of blockchain

Addressing the disadvantages of blockchain we have discussed above requires constant research, innovation, and of course, collaboration between developers. Most of these challenges are already being worked upon. For example, there are layer-1 and layer-2 blockchain solutions to share the workload of the main blockchain and solve for scalability issues, while there are ongoing efforts to address interoperability challenges through blockchain bridges, wrapped crypto, and more.

Despite the challenges, blockchain holds insurmountable potential to transform industries, foster trust, and create more efficient and secure systems across the world. Its increasing adoption and new developments in the field every few days are more than enough proof of that.

[Interlinking blockchain with AI and robotics](#contents.xhtml#sc3_53a)

Since we know quite a lot about the workings and prospects of blockchain already, let us take this opportunity to learn a little about how the technological collaborations we propose and discuss in this book might work.

Blockchain can be linked to robotics and AI in several ways that we can think of, creating brand new possibilities while also enhancing the capabilities of these technologies.

Here are some use cases for ways blockchain might be able to intersect with robotics and AI:

*   Data integrity: In robotic systems and AI models, data integrity is a very critical component for accurate decision-making. This is where blockchain’s immutability and transparency may come in to record data provenance and ensure the authenticity of the data being used in AI training datasets.
    
    Blockchain can store data hashes to help in tracing the history and lineage of datasets. This has two perks:
    
    *   Preventing data manipulation
    *   Enhancing the trust in AI systems that were previously discussed
*   Autonomous robotics and AI governance: While robots and AI systems become more autonomous, governance and accountability in these systems have become crucial factors in need of oversight. Blockchains can bring in a decentralized framework for robotics and AI governance where decisions made by AI systems pass through a consensus procedure and get recorded on the blockchain for transparency, easy audibility, and accountability.
*   AI and smart contracts: AI can be integrated into blockchain-based smart contracts to bring in the capability to handle more complex and automated decision-making processes. This would truly render any kind of human intervention at any point unnecessary, resulting in sped-up procedures. For a clearer understanding of how this will be achieved, further details will be provided in subsequent chapters.
*   AI for blockchain governance: AI can be used to analyze blockchain data and suggest optimizations for any network parameters as time changes, such as the consensus algorithms being used. AI-driven analytics can overall help improve the performance of blockchain networks.
*   AI and IoT integration: When we add the Internet of Things (IoT) to the mix, blockchain and AI can create a secure, decentralized ecosystem for connected devices. AI algorithms can now process data acquired from IoT devices, and then blockchain can take over to ensure secure communication between IoT devices, giving a boost to the overall reliability and security of IoT systems and making our daily lives a lot easier. 
*   AI-powered marketplaces: Blockchain-centered platforms can facilitate AI-based marketplaces, where developers may offer their AI models for use in a decentralized manner. All transactions would be executed through smart contracts, bringing in a smooth-running AI ecosystem.
*   Decentralized AI models and federated learning: Decentralized AI models leverage blockchain to host AI services on a distributed network of nodes, enhancing accessibility and reducing risks associated with central points of failure. This infrastructure supports federated learning, where AI models can be trained on distributed data sources without ever requiring sharing raw data. This solves privacy concerns in the training of AI models.

As is our overall goal to show you through the expanse of this book, the integration of AI, blockchain, and robotics presents many exciting opportunities across sectors while allowing for increased transparency, security, and efficiency.

Once we solve the scalability and privacy challenges and learn to address the ethical concerns by initiating responsible usage of AI and robotics in decentralized blockchains, the potential combinations of these technologies will essentially reshape most industries as we know them!

Now, let us talk about the final technology being discussed in this chapter: robotics and automation!

[Understanding robotics and automation](#contents.xhtml#sc2_54a)

On the final leg of this chapter, before we get into the fun examples, let us understand what robotics and automation all are about.

Automation and robotics are, of course, two different technologies. Using the terms interchangeably implies one’s lack of knowledge regarding both technologies. However, the two do overlap in various sectors to enable a range of smart procedures.

[Defining robotics and automation](#contents.xhtml#sc3_55a)

Robotics and automation are two frequently interconnected fields of technology that facilitate the design, creation, and operation of machines that can perform given tasks autonomously or semi-autonomously without requiring any human intervention.

Robotics is a branch of technology that encompasses the design, construction, and operation of robots. A robot is a machine or mechanical device that is programmable and capable of executing tasks autonomously or under the control of an operator.

Robots can be used in various industries like manufacturing, healthcare, agriculture, and deep sea or space exploration. Usually, robots are used to perform tasks too dangerous or repetitive for humans and increase efficiency and precision.

Automation, on the other hand, is the technology that allows control systems to put machinery to use with minimal or no human intervention. Automation is used to achieve streamlined processes, cut down on manual labor, and enhance productivity rates. These automated systems can be everything from turning on lights when sensors detect darkness to more complex procedures in finance, manufacturing, and logistics.

The combination of these two technologies can lead to increased efficiency, reduced labor costs, improved safety, and the capability to precisely perform tasks that may not be possible for humans across a broad range of industries.

Robotics and automation are advancing rapidly as intertwined technology, with further integration of AI, machine learning, blockchain, smart contracts, and more. 

Overall, these combinations lead to supremely sophisticated and capable systems.

[Categories of robotics and automation](#contents.xhtml#sc3_56a)

Robotics can be separated into three distinct and broad categories, namely:

*   Machines that are reliant on human input to operate.
*   Semi-autonomous robots that can perform some tasks by themself but still require human intervention and supervision.
*   Completely autonomous robots that possess the intelligence to perform given tasks entirely by themselves and can even respond to yet unseen situations.

Automation can also be segregated into three broad categories, which are:

*   Software automation, which makes use of a computer program to deal with repetitive tasks based in a virtual environment. 
*   Robotic process Automation (RPA), which is a type of software automation that can make use of a computer’s interface the way a human would. RPA tools may be made more efficient with AI and ML so they can perform tasks based on a given set of rules.
*   Industrial automation, where machines are enabled to carry out physical processes. This may include robotic automation.

[Robotics vs. automation](#contents.xhtml#sc3_57a)

[Table 2.3](#ch02.xhtml#tab2-3) lists the differences of robotics and automation side-by-side:

|  || Robotics || Automation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The basics || This is the branch of technology that deals with the design, construction, and operation of machines we call robots. A robot is a mechanical device that is programmable, and capable of executing tasks autonomously or under the control of an operator.  || Automation is the use of technology to operate machinery and systems with minimal human intervention. This technology aims to streamline processes, reduce manual labor, and improve productivity. |
| Components  || Robotics involves physical components such as sensors, actuators, motors, and mechanical frameworks. Further, other requirements involve software and algorithms to control the machines. || Automation involves mostly software systems, which include control systems, sensors, and actuators. These components might or might not be attached to physical robots. |
| Scope || The focus of robotics is specifically on the development and deployment of robots for various use cases. It involves the studies of robot mechanics, control systems, sensors, and programming to create machines that are capable of carrying out tasks that would otherwise require human intervention. || This one is a comparatively broader and more generic concept that encompasses any use of technology to automate any processes and tasks, and the scope here is not just limited to physical tasks.Automation can come to include software automation, industrial automation, and even business process automation. |
| Purpose || The purpose of this technology is to create machines which perform tasks in a similar manner as humans, most often in physical environments. Robots are designed to interact with the physical world in the first place, and they can be used in industries ranging from manufacturing to healthcare to space exploration. || The purpose here is to reduce human intervention in any tasks and processes. The purpose of automation is to optimize and accelerate operations, enhance efficiency, and bring any human errors to zero by employing the technology to control and manage systems in a human-free way, in both physical and virtual spaces. |
| Application || Robotics is already finding application in a range of industries and tasks that require physical interaction with the environment, such as surgery, exploration, and logistics. || This can be applied to a wide range of tasks, as mentioned—both physical and non-physical. Automation can be put to use in tasks like data entry, analysis for large volumes of data, scheduling things like meetings and maintenance procedures, monitoring of functions, and retaining control of industrial processes. |

Table 2.3: Robotics vs. automation

Now, on to the advantages and disadvantages of these technologies before we talk about combining them and automation in a variety of use cases.

[Advantages vs. disadvantages of robotics](#contents.xhtml#sc4_58a)

Robotics offers a broad range of benefits across various industries and applications which are as follows:

*   Advantages of robotics: 
    *   Robots are capable of performing most tasks faster and more consistently than humans, no matter how dangerous or repetitive. Naturally, this means increased productivity and efficiency in any processes you can imagine.
    *   Robots can make precise and accurate movements better than humans, which can be particularly useful in manufacturing, assembly, and many other tasks that require high levels of precision and are prone to falling prey to human errors otherwise.
    *   As mentioned, robots can perform dangerous or hazardous tasks with superb efficiency, which helps human workers avoid said activities.
    *   After the initial costs of implementation, robotics can actually lead to massive cost savings for businesses, since they can efficiently replace manual labor, reduce errors, and optimize all processes overall.
    *   Robots can be programmed and reprogrammed to perform different tasks with ease, which allows for scalability and adaptability as per changing needs of an industry and sector.
    *   Robots are operational 24/7 without pause, which allows for, again, very efficient operations and production.
    *   On the more humanitarian side, robots can assist individuals with disabilities and provide tireless support in healthcare, enhancing the quality of life for such patients and even easing the workload for their caregivers.
    *   Robots can further bring excellent educational tools and facilitate research in various domains to help out society as a whole.
    *   Robotics as a technology helps further innovation in relevant fields like AI and ML, thus playing an even more significant role in revolutionizing the future of various sectors.
*   Disadvantages of robotics: 
    *   The upfront investment in robotics can be significant, especially when it comes to advanced and specialized robotic systems. This makes it challenging for small and medium businesses to adopt the technology in the first place.
    *   These systems can be complex to maintain and require frequent programming based upon the industry’s needs. This hinders work procedures, not to mention adds up on the cost front.
    *   Robotics systems are usually programmed for specific tasks and do not possess enough flexibility or adaptability as humans to handle unfamiliar environments.
    *   While robotic systems are active 24/7, power outages can render them useless.
    *   Robots, since they happen to be machines connected to the internet or any other kind of network for communication and instructions, are vulnerable to cybersecurity threats.
    *   As with AI, there are ethical considerations here too: what if robots are used for military applications, for example? That can have disastrous consequences. On a smaller scale, the adoption of robotics in certain industries can lead to job displacement, creating an issue of mass unemployment.
    *   A lack of human touch can spell a less personalized and customized experience in various sectors, such as customer service.

[Table 2.4](#ch02.xhtml#tab2-4) shows the advantages and disadvantages of robotics, and all summed up:

| Advantages of robotics || Disadvantages of robotics |
| --- | --- |
| 

*   Robots can perform most tasks faster and more consistently than humans, leading to increased productivity and efficiency.
*   Robots can make precise and accurate movements better than humans, reducing scope for human errors.
*   Once implemented, robotics can lead to cost savings for businesses.
*   Robots can be programmed and reprogrammed to perform different tasks with ease, allowing for superior scalability.
*   Robots are operational 24/7 without pause.
*   Robots can assist individuals with disabilities and provide support in healthcare.
*   Robots can make educational tools globally accessible, and facilitate research in various domains.
*   Robotics helps advance other technologies like AI and ML.

 || 

*   The upfront investment in robotics can be significant, especially for smaller businesses.
*   Robotics systems can be complex to maintain, taking time and eating into a tight budget.
*   Robots lack the flexibility to adapt to different work environments.
*   Events like power outages can make robotic systems useless.
*   Robots are vulnerable to cybersecurity threats.
*   Ethical concerns involving misuse of robotic power and job displacement for the working class are prevalent.
*   A lack of human touch can make for a less personalized and customized experience.

 |

Table 2.4: Advantages and disadvantages of robotics

Addressing the disadvantages requires, once again, careful consideration on the part of us humans, proper planning, and responsible deployment of robotics technology. The best way to solve ethical concerns is to strike a balance between human and robotic capabilities.

This would further help to reach the full potential of robotics, because we believe a certain level of human involvement is always needed to achieve true greatness in any field. We are sure most of you would agree.

Next up, benefits and drawbacks of automation!

[Advantages vs. disadvantages of automation](#contents.xhtml#sc4_59a)

Automation, as we know, offers a wide range of advantages for virtually all businesses and industries.

*   Advantages of automation: 
    *   Automation as a technology streamline processes and does away with the need for human intervention, making for faster and more efficient operations in any field.
    *   Automation helps you save up on the cost spent on human labor, allowing for sizable savings in the long run.
    *   Automation is particularly useful for repetitive and labor-intensive tasks, of course.
    *   Automated systems can perform tasks with quite high precision as well as consistency, minimizing human errors that would otherwise happen, and improving overall quality of operations.
    *   Automated systems are active around the clock ceaselessly.
    *   Automated systems can be easily made to scale as per changing industry demands or production requirements, which certainly makes for a very scalable solution for businesses.
    *   Automation can of course handle dangerous tasks humans are incapable of accomplishing. This improves safety for the human counterparts in an operation.
    *   Automated systems can respond quicker than humans to preprogrammed triggers, reducing delays when it comes to a critical situation.
    *   Automated systems can process and analyze large loads of data quickly and with efficiency, proving great for faster decision-making and optimization of procedures.
    *   As automation takes over repetitive tasks, humans are free to focus on the more creative and/or strategic aspects of a particular kind of work. This makes for optimized productivity in a team.
    *   Automation can enhance customer service through personalized responses, chatbots, and more, ensuring faster support across industries.
    *   Automation can be integrated in technologies like AI and robotics to improve overall operations.
*   Disadvantages of automation: 
    *   Automation is another tech that raises concerns of job displacement for different sectors.
    *   Again, the implementation of automated systems for your business in the first place may require quite substantial investment infrastructure-wise, which prevents smaller businesses from accessing their benefits.
    *   Automation systems can be complex to maintain.
    *   Machines programmed to work a certain way with automation would struggle to adapt to any unexpected situation, requiring human intervention and lacking flexibility.
    *   Technical failures, cybersecurity threats, and even something as mundane as a power outage would bring an entire process involving automation to a halt for an indefinite amount of time.
    *   Over reliance on automation can cause errors that might be too late to fix, as well as disastrous consequences.
    *   Automation may limit or completely eliminate the creativity and innovation human workers can bring when it comes to problem solving or the generation of new ideas.
    *   A lack of human interaction can affect various sectors and lead to customer dissatisfaction, such as in customer service roles.

The quick of snapshot of the advantages and disadvantages of automation is shown in [Table 2.5](#ch02.xhtml#tab2-5):

| Advantages of automation || Disadvantages of automation |
| --- | --- |
| 

*   Automation streamlines processes and limits human intervention, increasing efficiency.
*   Automation reduces cost spent on human labor.
*   Automated systems are useful for repetitive and labor-intensive tasks.
*   Automated systems are active around the clock.
*   Automated systems can be highly scalable as per changing needs.
*   Automation can handle tasks too dangerous for humans.
*   Automated systems can respond quicker than humans to preprogrammed triggers.
*   Automation can enhance customer service through faster and personalized responses.
*   Automation can be integrated with technologies like AI and robotics for advancements in all these fields.

 || 

*   Automation raises concerns of job displacement for different sectors.
*   Smaller businesses may struggle with implementing automated systems due to the high upfront costs.
*   Automation systems are complex to maintain.
*   Automated systems have low flexibility to adapt to changing requirements.
*   Technical failures and cybersecurity threats plagues these systems.
*   Over reliance on automation can have irreversible consequences.
*   Automation limits human creativity and innovation.
*   Lack of human interaction can make for unsatisfactory experiences for users in sectors like customer service.

 |

Table 2.5: Advantages vs. disadvantages of automation

Both robotics and automation share similarities in terms of benefits and disadvantages, and again, addressing all these disadvantages of automation is very possible with careful planning and effective management so as to maintain a balance of human and automated workforces.

Now we get to how AI, automation, blockchain, and robotics combine to bring various real-world use cases.

[Examples for AI, blockchain, robotics and automation](#contents.xhtml#sc2_60a)

The best approach to explain the applications of these technologies individually and through combos to you would be to do it through fascinating examples we have already seen. Let us jump straight into these examples!

[Robot nurse Grace](#contents.xhtml#sc3_61a)

Grace was created by a Hong-Kong-based robotics company to make patients feel relaxed and comfortable. Grace was introduced as a sister to the world-renowned Sophia, an android, to be granted citizenship in Saudi Arabia. Grace was born during the coronavirus pandemic in 2021, and she was a fusion of AI, robotics, and automated procedures.

[Bionic limbs](#contents.xhtml#sc3_62a)

Bionic limbs are electromechanical devices that attach to the human body to replace a limb, like an arm or a hand. Bionic user control systems attempt to intercept the brain’s signals that would otherwise be interpreted by the limbs they replace and convert them into commands for the device. This would again involve AI and automation.

[Vision-based AI drones](#contents.xhtml#sc3_63a)

Autonomous drones from the University of Zurich challenged the best human pilots in the world to a fair drone race last year, where both relied on their visual perception and capabilities of thought and response to the situation at hand. There was some wonderful usage of AI, automation, and relevant technologies involved here, which led to the drones winning the race. Please refer to the following figure:

![](images/Figure_2.2.jpg)

Figure 2.2: World Drone Prix, Dubai \[Creative Commons License\]

[Fetch.ai](#contents.xhtml#sc3_64a)

Fetch.ai is a platform that is going to help you automate mundane tasks like booking a flight and can even be used for financial guidance in the decentralized finance space. Fetch.ai decentralizes and automates web transactions by deploying a bot as your digital twin which interacts with digital twins from airline providers or other traders/ financial service providers and fetches a deal using parameters you have set.

[Shape-changing microrobots in healthcare](#contents.xhtml#sc3_65a)

Microrobots can be used to transport drugs such as those involved in chemotherapy directly to cancer cells, as tested by researchers Jiawen Li, Li Zhang, Dong Wu, and more. This can be pathbreaking in fighting cancer. Involved technologies include robotics, automation, and AI.

[AI-Blockchain](#contents.xhtml#sc3_66a)

This New York-based platform brings a digital asset cloud protocol built upon blockchain and AI. The organization applies AI agents to its blockchain to ensure security. The goal for this organization is to bring scalable and tamper-free database solutions to businesses to support a range of operations.

[Home robots as personal assistants](#contents.xhtml#sc3_67a)

Labrador Systems recently introduced Retriever, a semi-autonomous mobile table that can transport objects based on human commands. This is a fantastic application of robotics and automation to help those with mobility challenges.

[Bext360](#contents.xhtml#sc3_68a)

Based in Denver, Colorado, this organization uses AI and blockchain to enhance supply chain transparency for coffee, timber, seafood, and mineral sectors. The AI involved analyzes crops, predicts growing patterns, while blockchain keeps records of a product’s journey throughout the supply chain- from seeds to finished products. Further, the blockchain even acts as a payments recorder and ensures all involved parties receive their payments/products quickly and transparently.

[Remote tattoo by robot arm](#contents.xhtml#sc3_69a)

T-Mobile Netherlands took the promotion of its 5G networks to a whole new level by developing an automated system for the world’s first remote tattoo. The tattoo was needled on actress Stijn Fransen by a robot arm being controlled by artist Wes Thomas in another location, all with the power of their 5G network. This process was recorded in a documentary-style film, involving robotics and automation.

The following figure shows the process of a tattoo being made by a robot:

![](images/Figure_2.3.png)

Figure 2.3: Remote tattoo

[BurstIQ’s LifeGraph](#contents.xhtml#sc3_70a)

This platform brings a combination of AI and blockchain to bring amplified ownership and management control to patients over their health data. LifeGraph allows healthcare professionals to build secure storage solutions for health records and wellness regimes for their patients. All applications that are built through this platform have features of consent and recovery-built in.

[Dobby](#contents.xhtml#sc3_71a)

Dobby, based in Washington, D.C., is a home services platform that makes use of both blockchain and AI, again. Homeowners can utilize this platform to look for assistance with maintenance and repair projects. The app for this platform connects local workers for roofing, plumbing, and other maintenance services to those seeking them out. Blockchain facilitates the quick exchange of data here and also helps reduce financial losses.

From their varied natures, as well as their diverse industries, we are sure you have a good idea of how inclusive and multi-faceted the implementations of the real-world applications of the technologies can be. Powered by technologies as powerful as AI, blockchain, robotics, and automation—that literally allow you to achieve any functionality your dream of—the sky is truly the limit.

[Conclusion](#contents.xhtml#sc2_72a)

While there are scalability, cost issues, and ethical issues attached to emerging technologies, it is only a matter of time before we humans come up with solutions to combat these challenges. The key is to stay empathetic to the human cause and make considerations to build teams with equal parts of contribution from human workers and smart systems, so efficiency and productivity remain intact while the creative juices keep flowing. Further, measures need to be taken to address issues of privacy and security.

In the next chapter, we will explore AI as a technology—where it came from, what it has accomplished, what lies in wait on the horizon for AI.

[Questions](#contents.xhtml#sc2_73a)

1.  How does AI work?
    
    Answer: Here are the steps for how AI works:
    
    *   Data collection: AI systems first have to be fed data that would work as a reference for their responses in certain circumstances.
    *   Data preprocessing: After collecting the needed data, an AI model puts this data through preprocessing to clean and make it suitable for the algorithm’s comprehension.
    *   Model building: AI algorithms then create models using the preprocessed data.
    *   Training: The preprocessed data is now used for the AI models to learn from it.
    *   Validation and testing: Once trained, the AI models are tested in their respective scenarios.
    *   Prediction: Now, the AI models are ready to be given inputs to produce desired outputs.
2.  What are the categories of AI?
    
    Answer: The two main categories of AI are:
    
    *   Strong AI, powerful on par with the cognitive abilities of a human brain. It can perform tasks across multiple domains and adapt with its own ability to think.
    *   Weak AI can only complete domain-specific tasks and cannot adapt to new scenarios.
3.  Name a few advantages and disadvantages of AI.
    
    Answer: Advantages of AI include:
    
    *   Can process huge volumes of data in little time and learn from it, enabling quick and efficient decision-making.
    *   Can automate mundane tasks.
    *   Helps cut down on labor costs.
    *   Can keep humans from venturing into potentially hazardous terrains.
    *   Can enhance user experience across platforms with personalized attention and recommendations.
    *   AI models can continuously learn and improve themselves to become more adaptable in changing circumstances.
    
    Disadvantages of AI include:
    
    *   Training these models can be expensive and cost an unprecedented amount of energy.
    *   Biased or wrong data can create incorrect or unfair outputs.
    *   Training data for AI brings concerns related to user privacy and data security.
    *   AI may eliminate human jobs, resulting in widespread unemployment and an alarming shift in the global sociopolitical structure.
4.  What are some prevalent applications of AI?
    
    Answer: AI has up-and-coming use cases in the following sectors:
    
    *   In healthcare AI is used for precise diagnosis, personalized care, remote monitoring and chatbots as health assistants, and more.
    *   AI is used in e-commerce to bring a customized shopping experience for users.
    *   AI can be used in agriculture to tend to crops and automate farming equipment.
5.  How can blockchain be combined with other emerging technologies like AI and robotics?
    
    Answer: There are several ways for the three technologies to come together. For example, blockchain and AI can come together to enhance traceability on the supply chain, while robotics can be employed to aid human workers and increase productivity.
    
6.  How are robotics and automation different?
    
    Answer: Robotics handles the design, construction, and operation of machines called robots. On the other hand, automation is just the technology that automates machines and systems so they can perform with little to no human intervention. So, we can say robotic systems use automation, but automated systems do not necessarily require the help of robotics.
    
7.  What are the ethical concerns of AI, robotics, and automation? How do you think we can solve these issues?
    
    Answer: An AI system with biased or wrong data used in training can provide unfair outputs, and wherever technologies like AI, robotics, and automation are involved, human workers may get sidelined and eliminated, resulting in an unemployment crisis. Further, AI systems, in particular, consume a lot of energy in the training process, which is yet another angle developers must try to solve.
    
8.  What are some possible use cases of a combination of blockchain, AI, and robotics?
    
    Answer: In healthcare, blockchain can be used to maintain patient records and make them easily accessible to healthcare providers. Meanwhile, AI-powered systems can provide emergency care, personalized treatment plans, and more, while robot nurses can be assigned to care for patients and participate in surgeries with the utmost precision.
