Moreover, automation in digital transaction settlements reduces paperwork and makes the process more efficient.
    
*   Blockchain for instant claims processing: Traditional claims processing requires a lengthy process, and settling a claim may take weeks usually. To increase the speed of claims processing and decrease the hassle of waiting, blockchains and smart contracts can once again be put to use.
    
    Real-world example: Etherisc is making use of blockchain to automate claims processing, by creating smart contracts which enforce an agreement when specific predetermined conditions are met. Third-party data providers are there to ensure the payment agreement’s terms are satisfied. So, after alerting the relevant insurance provider, Etherisc reviews the initial claim investigation and policy checking procedures, increasing efficiency and reducing required time for claims settlement.
    
*   Blockchain for food product quality check: There are many instances of popular companies making use of blockchain-based applications to ensure the food they bring to end users are fresh. In case of any issues reported, blockchains ensure that the product in question can be traced back to its origin, and the problem can be identified and solved as early as possible.
    
    Real-world example: One of these examples would be Nestlé, who wanted to reassure Chinese parents about the excellent quality of their instant formula NAN A2. In 2008, about 3,00,000 babies fell sick in China with melamine from similar products, which is why Nestlé needed a strategy to earn back the parents’ trust.
    
    Nestlé partnered with Chinese technology firm Techrock to create a public blockchain application that can be accessed through a mobile app. This brought heightened transparency and allowed parents to verify the NAN A2’s ingredients, source and production details, and packaging information. This led to Nestlé holding the biggest share within the infant nourishment market in China.
    
*   Blockchain for energy distribution: We have discussed the usage of blockchain’s decentralized technology in energy distribution before. There are times when the supply and demand of energy are not in balance, and optimization of electricity distribution is a dire need. Blockchain can help with peer-to-peer distribution of energy in such times.
    
    Real-world example: TenneT is an energy transmission operator based in the Netherlands and Germany. The firm partnered with IBM and Sonnen for a blockchain application to optimize the power grid. Now, energy storage systems could link to TenneT’s power grid database. The distributed mechanism allows any imbalances in the supply and demand of electricity to be shared with involved stakeholders. Then, the application allows the connected energy storages to collect or release energy as needed, making energy distribution efficient.
    
*   Blockchain for tracking and sharing intellectual property: There are quite a few businesses that lack the opportunity to share the true value of their assets with possible investors, resulting in them being undervalued. Blockchain can be used to tokenize intellectual property and transfer it easily and transparently with intended parties, however. Alongside, blockchain also verifies the authenticity of this IP and tracks it from its source to anyone viewing the tokens’ contents.
    
    Real-world example: IPwe has partnered with IBM to bring a blockchain that lets IP from businesses be tokenized and stored in the cloud. This way, potential investors can easily access an IP and invest after verifying its data. So, all businesses now have a fair chance to advertise their intellectual property, and investing also becomes simpler.
    

With that, a list of blockchain applications across industries with well-known case studies comes to an end. As you can deduce, the transparency and decentralization of blockchain helps add the trust factor in most of its use cases, along with increasing accessibility to data for all parties involved.

Time to explore some use cases for robotics and automation.

[Applications of robotics and automation and case studies](#contents.xhtml#sc3_317a)

Robotics and automation find applications across a range of sectors to improve efficiency, precision, and safety in various processes. They are either put to work by themselves, or they can even be programmed to work alongside their human counterparts in various jobs. Some common industry use cases of robotics include:

*   Robotics and automation in assembly and dispensing: Robotic arms and automation systems are widely used in manufacturing plants for tasks such as assembling, welding, painting, and packaging. They enhance both speed and accuracy, leading to increased production efficiency.
    
    | Note: Assembly robots usually do not move around, and are fastened to the floor or locked into space through some other means. |
    | --- |
    
    Further, there are dispensing robots for jobs like applying adhesives, nailing and stapling, wiring, cabling, screwdriving, and more as part of different applications. These tasks require agile robots.
    
*   Robotics and automation in handling and logistics: Robots are frequently employed in warehouses and distribution centers for monotonous tasks that require high accuracy, like picking, packing, and sorting goods. Automated guided vehicles (AGVs) and autonomous mobile robots (AMRs) are there to optimize material flow and reduce manual labor as well as costs.
*   Robotics and automation in welding and metal fabrication: Robots are extensively used in welding and metal fabrication processes. They are programmed to ensure consistent weld quality and are equipped to handle repetitive tasks in harsh or hazardous environments.
*   Robotics and automation in packaging and palletizing: Robotics can streamline packaging processes by automating tasks like placing items into boxes, sealing, labeling, and palletizing—again, monotonous jobs that demand precision, nonetheless. Employing robots and introducing automation improves efficiency and reduces errors in packaging operations.
*   Robotics and automation in painting and coating: Robots are again employed for precision painting and coating applications across industries such as automotive manufacturing. Robots are programmed to ensure uniform coating, reduce waste, and enhance the quality of finished products.
*   Robotics and automation in quality inspection: Vision systems and robotic arms can be brought together for quality control and inspection tasks. Robots can examine products for defects, measure dimensions, and ensure adherence to quality standards.
*   Robotics and automation in food processing: In the food industry, robots are put to use for tasks like sorting, cutting, and packaging. This is especially beneficial in the food industry because hygiene is enhanced, contamination risks are reduced, and they improve overall food safety.
*   Robotics and automation in pharmaceutical manufacturing: Robots play a role in pharmaceutical production as well for tasks like dispensing, sorting, and packaging medications. In an industry with strict regulatory requirements, automation brought by robots can ensure heightened compliance.
*   Robotics and automation in construction and demolition: Robotic systems can be employed in construction work to perform tasks like bricklaying, concrete spraying, and demolition. Robots thus enhance safety for human workers and can handle repetitive or hazardous tasks.
*   Robotics and automation in mining and exploration: Autonomous robots and drones are used in mining operations for tasks like exploration, surveying, and transportation of materials. They improve safety and efficiency in challenging environments.
*   Robotics and automation in agriculture: Agricultural robots, including drones and autonomous tractors, can be used for tasks such as planting, harvesting, and monitoring crops. They can even be programmed to recognize fragile crops and handle them with extra care. Automation magnifies efficiency in agricultural tasks and reduces the need for manual labor.

All of this industry use cases of robotics, and automation demonstrates the versatility and benefits of the technology in enhancing productivity, precision, as well as safety across various sectors.

As all aforementioned use cases of AI, blockchain, and robotics and automation show, the technologies have taken over numerous industries to provide benefits of cost reduction, enhanced workflow, magnified transparency, efficient compliance, increased security, decreased dependence on human resources and in turn reduced instances of errors, and more. However, despite all such benefits, there remain ethical and legal issues that require attention from developers and, in some cases, regulatory authorities. The next part of the chapter will explore such issues.

[Ethical and legal issues surrounding the technologies of tomorrow](#contents.xhtml#sc2_318a)

There are several challenges involved in the applications of blockchain, AI, robotics, and automation. While we have discussed most of them before, here is an overview of some key concerns and how they can create roadblocks to widespread accessibility:

*   Immutability and privacy concerns of blockchain: Blockchain’s immutability adds to the security of data stored on the platform, but there are issues stemming from the feature, too. Once data is added to the blockchain, it is challenging to modify or erase. This means if you accidentally put any private data on the chain, you would not be able to erase it, which could potentially lead to privacy violations.
    
    Such privacy concerns may hinder the adoption of blockchain, especially in applications where data confidentiality is paramount.
    
*   Lack of opportunities and equity brought by AI: When it comes to AI, and robotics and automation too for that matter, the technologies have the potential to displace certain jobs (especially those requiring manual labor), leading to unemployment and contributing to economic inequality. Without proper measures in place from developers, organizations, and legislators, the fear of job loss and unequal access to opportunities can slow down the acceptance of AI and robotics. This is particularly true for marginalized communities with limited opportunities already.
*   Job displacement brought by robotics and automation: As mentioned, while robotics-based automation increasingly takes over industries, certain manual jobs would be passed on to them or become obsolete, impacting employment opportunities for certain sectors of the workforce. Without appropriate regulations and proactive measures from employers, the fear of job displacement can lead to resistance against the heavy adoption of robotics in industries, particularly in regions of the world heavily reliant on traditional labor.
*   Disintermediation and lack of accountability brought by blockchain: Blockchain's disintermediation eliminates the need for intermediaries, allowing for lower costs and more control over their information and finances to users. However, this can also pose challenges in terms of accountability, especially when any unprecedented issues arise. When you add in the AI element, a lack of clear accountability mechanisms and potential biases in artificial intelligence-powered decision-making can lead to skepticism and hinder widespread acceptance. Moreover, the shift of power away from traditional intermediaries may be met with resistance from existing power structures. However, the former at least can be addressed by enabling user education regarding the up-and-coming technologies and conscious choices from developers to ensure transparency in AI decision-making and take accountability for any issues arising.
*   Bias and unfairness brought by AI: As we know, AI algorithms may inherit any biases present in training data fed to them. This could lead to unfair outputs that could be wrong and reinforce existing societal/other prejudices. Therefore, biased AI can create an atmosphere of mistrust and resistance, resisting the deployment of all AI applications by perceiving them as unjust or discriminatory. Once again, clear policies of accountability and transparency among developers can ensure this issue is abolished at the very root.
*   Opacity brought by AI: Some AI models keep their training data sets hidden, which can, of course, lead to concerns about accountability and fairness. This lack of transparency will most certainly lead to resistance and stakeholders demanding visibility into the decision-making processes.
*   Regulatory non-compliance: The rapid development of technologies like blockchain, AI, and robotics is outpacing regulatory frameworks, which can lead to challenges in ensuring compliance with ethical standards and legal requirements. As you can deduce, regulatory uncertainties are bound to impede the widespread adoption of all upcoming technologies as businesses and individuals hesitate due to concerns about potential legal ramifications. The only solution to this one is for regulatory authorities all over the globe to bring sector-specific regulations while developers and all stakeholders in the technological industries come together to adopt a set of guidelines ensuring transparency, privacy, and accountability to repel the ethical and other issues currently in the way of the digital future.

Facing and solving all these challenges will be crucial for the technologies of tomorrow to move forward. And as we near the inevitable era of AI, blockchain, robotics, and all-around automation, it is important to have the foresight to predict and plan for future challenges.

[Challenges and roadblocks for the future](#contents.xhtml#sc2_319a)

As technologies like AI, robotics, and blockchain evolve, the future challenges they might encounter include:

*   Striking the right balance between innovation and ethical practices will be a continuous challenge, not something that can be solved in one go. It will be important for developers to continually upgrade themselves accordingly.
*   Cybersecurity challenges are another field where we have to be vigil at all times. The more integrated AI, robotics, and blockchain are in critical systems, the higher the risk of cybersecurity attacks. Adopting and updating robust security measures to protect against data breaches, hacking, and other malicious activities will therefore have to be another ongoing process.
*   Preparing existing workforces for transitions and upskilling them will be required to address potential inequalities in the job market.
*   Ensuring seamless interoperability between different technologies and platforms will be challenging as ecosystems beget more components and become increasingly complex. In blockchain, developers are already working to solve this challenge, and other sectors will need continued innovation here as well.
*   The energy consumption of AI models, especially when it comes to deep learning, can be significant. Addressing the environmental impact and working towards more energy-efficient algorithms will be a challenge to overcome, that too while the scale and complexity of AI applications grow.

Addressing challenges like the ethical use of AI, blockchain, and robotics, setting international standards, and managing global cybersecurity threats require collaboration between global governments, as well as industries and organizations. Building such collaborative frameworks could be another complex task.

Of course, effectively navigating these challenges will require a focused effort from technology developers, policymakers, businesses, and even the common man. However, with proactive measures, ethical considerations, and ongoing innovation, any issue can be solved with passing time.

[Conclusion](#contents.xhtml#sc2_320a)

We have navigated through the use cases of AI, blockchain, and robotics and automation across industries, and learned about how they aid privacy, transparency, efficiency, and more across industries. However, we have also learned about ethical and legal issues plaguing the technologies of tomorrow. As we have deduced, addressing such challenges requires a combined effort among technologists, developers, regulators, and society at large. Establishing clear ethical guidelines, ensuring transparency and accountability, promoting inclusivity, and developing robust regulatory frameworks are all equally essential steps to overcome all of these roadblocks and enable the responsible and equitable deployment of blockchain, AI, and robotics technologies.

In the next chapter, we will discuss building the future we have envisioned so far. From educating every individual to building communities to ethical decision-making and sustainable practices to adopt, let us find out all about how to build a technologically empowered future!

[Questions](#contents.xhtml#sc2_321a)

1.  What are some industrial applications of AI?
    
    Answer: AI in manufacturing process optimization and predictive maintenance are two prominent industrial applications of AI.
    
2.  How can blockchain empower different industries?
    
    Answer: Blockchain can enhance supply chains with greater transparency and smooth workflow and even make international payments easier.
    
3.  How do robotics and automation benefit different industries?
    
    Answer: Robotics and automation are needed across a range of industries, for things like handling and logistics, and assembly and dispensing.
    
4.  What are some ethical and legal issues surrounding the implementation of up-and-coming technologies? How do you think they can be solved?
    
    Answer: There are privacy concerns surrounding blockchain and job displacement brought forth by AI and robotics, all of which can be solved by careful and precise consideration on the part of developers and users.
    
5.  What would be important measures for developers to take to ensure an ethical and considerate stance with AI and other technologies?
    
    Answer: Developers can come together and create a set of common guidelines to allow for the ethical considerations to be removed.
    

# [CHAPTER 12Building the Future](#contents.xhtml#ch12a)

[Introduction](#contents.xhtml#sc2_322a)

At long last, we are near the end of our journey in this book. With the many use cases of AI, blockchain, robotics, and automation we have discussed so far—both on their own and converged with the other emerging technologies—we must already have a good idea of what the future looks like. However, to live a convenient and efficient life tomorrow, there are still some challenges to be solved today relevant to said emerging technologies. We have discussed some of these challenges briefly before, but now it is time for a comprehensive look at the issues that remain to be solved.

From education for the common man regarding the new technologies and their benefits and perils, to building community engagement, to ethical decision-making on the part of stakeholders, to maintaining sustainable practices—these are only a few of the factors we have to keep in mind today to build a better tomorrow. So, how to find solutions here? Today, AI, blockchain and robotics are converging to reshape the future of technology and business models across various sectors. This convergence streamlines operations and also opens up avenues for innovation. But, despite renewable energy being a dire necessity for these technologies to succeed, what challenges remain on that front, and how do we solve them? Let us explore it all.

[Structure](#contents.xhtml#sc2_323a)

The chapter will cover the following topics:

*   Foreseeing into tomorrow
*   Primary challenges to tackle
*   Solutions to the most pressing challenges
*   Challenges and solutions: Green energy

[Objectives](#contents.xhtml#sc2_324a)

In this chapter, our objective is to learn how the future of the technologies will look like. Further, we will explore the challenges today which are keeping those visions from being realized—we will go from exploring issues with individual empowerment and community engagement to problems in ethical decision-making and sustainable practices. Further, we will discover why exactly these aspects are so critical for the emerging technologies like blockchain, AI, robotics, and automation, and explore some solutions to the aforementioned issues. Finally, to top it all off, we will also explore the challenges in the mainstream adoption of green energy—as it is intricately tied to the technologies of tomorrow- and the solutions for those issues.

[Foreseeing into tomorrow](#contents.xhtml#sc2_325a)

The convergence of artificial intelligence, blockchain, robotics and automation with other cutting-edge technologies like IoT powered by green energy will lead to complete transformation across industries and even our daily lives. The world as we know it stands to be reshaped, and here are just a few things the world might normalize tomorrow:

*   AI-driven analytics would be employed for valuable insights across fields, which would allow businesses, for example, to make better data-powered decisions, optimize all business procedures, and magnify workflow efficiency overall. Further, in healthcare, AI-driven data analytics can be used to detect diseases, formulate personalized diet and treatment plans, and overall increase patient experiences while also enhancing privacy.
*   Further, AI-fueled medical imaging systems can help doctors detect diseases like cancer more efficiently and at early stages. Further, AI-powered chatbots can be used for quick medical advice and emergency healthcare support. Overall, the technologies of tomorrow will increase accessibility for the underserved population of the globe.
*   Speaking of healthcare, there can be shared blockchains for healthcare professionals and hospitals where patient records are stored and can be easily pulled up at any time by nodes, reducing paperwork and waiting periods. Assisted robots are already performing surgical procedures with the utmost precision, potentially saving more lives in the process by removing any possible human errors.
*   Quantum computing, advanced AI chips, and specialized AI-based hardware will become ubiquitous, more energy-efficient and enable major breakthroughs via AI algorithms and applications.
*   AI and robotics will bring intelligent machines across every sector of our lives. For example, across industries like logistics, manufacturing, and agriculture, smart robots will be employed for precise and rapid workflows. Warehouses can employ smart robots to optimize order fulfillment procedures, reducing human efforts (and thus human errors) and delivery times. This would make customer experiences better and lead to better cost savings.
*   Improved user experience is certainly the ultimate point of the technological evolution we currently witness, and that is exactly what tomorrow’s technologies pave the path for as well. AI, blockchain, and robotics bring a degree of personalization to user experiences never witnessed before. We get a hint of it already when Netflix somehow recommends the exact movie we want to watch or when Amazon recommends products that are bound to catch our eye and match our taste. The tailored recommendations are made possible through AI algorithms that scan our browsing history and preferences to gauge exactly what we like. While our experience is enhanced, businesses also ensure loyalty through this improvement.
*   AI and IoT combine to magnify the scope of intelligent, online devices. Such devices can learn from our behavior, habits, and preferences just as well as streaming services or e-commerce websites, and they can adapt to provide better user experience accordingly. In the future, there will be smart homes where IoT and AI enabled devices combine with AI-powered voice assistants to do everything from controlling temperature to fortifying security systems to restocking our pantry. Living would be even more convenient than sci-fi books of the past could possibly have imagined.
*   AI-powered vehicles would bring smart, autonomous cars for the future. Self-driving cars can reduce road accidents and lower traffic congestion—this is because AI-fueled machines are not prone to emotional outbursts and are therefore better at following rules. Further, AI can also bring forth mobility solutions for the elderly and those with disabilities.
*   Blockchain will continue to ensure transparent and secure transactions, fostering trust in financial dealings, supply chains, and any digital interactions at all.
*   Green energy will be made more accessible and pocket-friendly, thereby becoming the primary source of energy for future generations. Meanwhile, AI will optimize energy consumption, and blockchain will ensure transparent energy transactions, while robotics can work to enhance the overall workflow at power plants.
*   Decentralized work models supported by blockchain will facilitate remote collaboration, with AI enhancing productivity and automation streamlining tasks at organizations, paving the path for the smoothest possible workflows that put human workers up to the tasks they like best while automating the repetitive and manual labor.
*   AI-driven personalized education platforms will allow us to learn at our own pace, setting our own goals and moving on to the next level when we deem fit. There can be blockchain-verified credentials to ensure the authenticity of our degrees and to streamline hiring/admission processes. Meanwhile, robotics can also contribute to teaching, revolutionizing the education landscape.
*   AI, blockchain, and robotics will collaborate in environmental monitoring, conservation efforts, and sustainable resource management. Green energy usage would push it a notch further, making these environmentally friendly.
*   While blockchain ensures data integrity, artificial intelligence can enhance cybersecurity, and robotics can play a role in securing physical and digital spaces, bolstering overall security both online and offline.
*   Reinforcement learning will be able to train complex AI models to enable more natural and intelligent interactions in real-time environments. This self-supervised learning will reduce the need for manual training and data analysis.
*   We will be interacting with more AI-robots than humans in the near-future. These can be in the form of tutors, career counsellors, chatbots, customer services, and more.
*   In the coming decade, we also expect significant advancements in humanoid robots, powered by AI technology. These robots will be human-like and trained to perform tasks in factories, hospitals, corporate companies, agriculture, etc. Companies like Tesla, Figure, and 1X Technologies are the forefront of this technology and plan to deploy such robots in the real-world environments soon.

Aside from these, we can also expect robust ethical frameworks and policies from governments all over the world to watch over tech development, ensuring responsible AI systems, fair blockchain practices, and ethical use of robotics.

Now, we know what promises tomorrow holds when AI, blockchain, robotics, and other technologies of the future are put to the best possible use. However, there are several downsides to these technological advancements.

One of the main concerns of integrating such high-end technologies are scalability issues. The inherent challenges of blockchain may hinder real-time data analysis of the AI and affect its performance. Since the regulatory guidelines and ethical considerations are continuously evolving, data privacy and security is another major drawback. While blockchain prioritizes security and anonymity, AI requires access to big data sets.

[Primary challenges to tackle](#contents.xhtml#sc2_326a)

As time goes by, there will always be new challenges to meet. However, for the world of today, here are the issues that must be addressed on priority for the technologies of tomorrow to reach the common man’s door:

*   Human factor: empowering individuals: Ensuring that the emerging technologies benefit individuals rather than creating inequalities or concentration of power is a responsibility that lies with developers and firms backing innovative endeavors in general. Empowerment of users of future fosters inclusivity, equal access to opportunities, and a more democratic distribution of the benefits of technology overall.
*   Bringing in community engagement: Involving communities in decision-making processes related to the deployment of AI, blockchain, robotics, and automation is both a key need and a crucial challenge for developers at this point in time. However, community engagement ensures that technological advancements align with local needs, values, and sustainability goals, fostering social acceptance and reducing resistance. This is one of the most important challenges to address to ensure mainstream acceptance for the technologies of tomorrow.
*   Ensuring ethical decision making: To ensure accountability and transparency to prevent disputes and/or take decisive actions upon a mishap, establishing ethical frameworks for the responsible development and use of AI, blockchain, robotics, automation, and other technologies is a must. Today, we also see a lot of AI hallucinations and deep fake concerns on the rise. AI hallucinations are false or misleading results generated by the AI model due to numerous underlying factors such as insufficient training data, incorrect assumptions or predictions, and data biases. Such AI models can create and spread convincing deep fake videos or audios of false events. In fact, ethical considerations made by developers proactively can bring a safeguard against unintended consequences, AI hallucinations and deep fakes, privacy violations, bias, and negative societal impacts, promoting a tomorrow that aligns with human values.
*   Importance of sustainable practices: Balancing technological innovation with environmental sustainability in the development and deployment of AI, blockchain, robotics, and automation is another immediate necessity. As we know, sustainable practices mitigate ecological impacts, contribute to a cleaner environment, and ensure the long-term viability of emerging technologies. Of course, renewable energies are one of the many ways to bring sustainability into the workings of the technologies of the future.
    
    Why are these four factors in particular so important? The reasons are many, including:
    
    *   Empowering individuals and engaging communities ensure that the benefits of technology are widespread, avoiding the creation of digital divides.
    *   Ethical decision-making fosters user trust in technology, encouraging its responsible adoption and reducing concerns about potential negative consequences.
    *   Prioritizing sustainable practices ensures that technological advancements contribute to, rather than harm, the overall well-being of the planet and its inhabitants.
    *   Adhering to all of these factors promotes a harmonious integration of futuristic technology into society, avoiding conflicts and fostering a positive societal impact.

[Solutions for the most pressing challenges](#contents.xhtml#sc2_327a)

Now we know which particular challenges demand immediate action to popularize the technologies of tomorrow and make them more accessible to the common man. Now, what solutions could there be? Let us discuss them in detail.

[Empowering the general populace](#contents.xhtml#sc3_328a)

First challenge to be addressed: how to empower the common man to adapt to the blockchain, AI, robotics, and other emerging technologies-powered future? The learning curve would be steep, and it would not be easy to convince people to move over to entirely new systems when they know next to nothing about it. This is why it is important to make them understand the benefits of a decentralized, automated future, and empower them with relevant knowledge.

Here is a starting point:

*   As stakeholders in the emerging technologies space, promote accessible and inclusive educational programs focusing on AI, blockchain, robotics, and automation to equip individuals with relevant skills for the future job markets and lifestyle.
*   Implement and initiate digital literacy programs that demystify emerging technologies, providing individuals with the basic knowledge to navigate and leverage these technologies and relevant tools effectively.
*   Forge partnerships with local organizations, schools, and community centers to disseminate information about upcoming technologies and facilitate engagement at the grassroots level.
*   Launch public awareness campaigns to demystify old myths and misunderstandings, address public concerns, and highlight the potential positive impact of emerging technologies.
*   Advocate for affordable and widespread access to technological devices and internet connectivity, bridging the digital divide and ensuring equal opportunities.
*   Foster community dialogues to include diverse perspectives in any decision-making processes related to technology implementation, ensuring representation and inclusivity.
*   Further, initiate conversations about diversity and inclusion in the tech industry to ensure a wide range of perspectives and experiences contribute to the development process of emerging technologies.
*   Integrate ethical considerations into AI and robotics training to empower individuals, ensuring ethical decision-making in technology use.
*   Promote blockchain-based financial services by making the application/website user interfaces more interactive and overall user experience smoother to enhance financial inclusion, allowing individuals in underserved areas to access banking and economic opportunities.
*   Empower individuals to understand and protect their digital privacy, advocating for policies that prioritize user data protection in AI, blockchain, and automation systems.
*   Support and/or initiate the creation of collaborative learning platforms where individuals can share experiences, insights, and best practices related to emerging technologies. This way, the experienced can provide assurance to the newcomers regarding new technologies and make their learning curves less steep.
*   Promote the adoption of inclusive design practices in technology development and create a common framework for the same with fellow team leads in the same technological niche. This step will ensure that all upcoming products and services are accessible to individuals with diverse abilities and backgrounds.
*   Establish reskilling and upskilling programs to support individuals in transitioning to new roles as automation changes the job landscape. There are many certified digital courses and training programs available to upgrade AI, blockchain, and robotics skills. Google, Coursera, Udemy, and several such platforms have tied up with reputed universities to offer leading courses on such advanced technologies.
*   Educate individuals about their legal rights in the digital space, empowering them to demand ethical and responsible practices from technological service providers.
*   For governments and regulatory bodies, it is possible to encourage entrepreneurship by providing specific support for startup ventures and small businesses leveraging emerging technologies, fostering innovation and economic growth. Further, this would help the common man place their trust in new technologies.

Empowering individuals in the face of emerging technologies is not an easy task, and it requires a multifaceted approach that accounts for education, accessibility, ethical considerations on our part, and encouraging active participation in shaping the future technological landscape.

[Increasing community engagement](#contents.xhtml#sc3_329a)

The approach to solving this challenge is not vastly different from empowering future users of technological marvels to adapt to them. However, this step does come after the general populace has been made aware of the basics of the emerging technologies, so the community would be less wary of the change.

Here is how we think it can be done:

*   As project developers in the emerging technologies sector, we can conduct workshops and webinars to educate our project community members about the benefits, risks, and applications of blockchain/ AI/ robotics/ automation/ other technologies we are working with.
*   We can opt for organizing live demonstrations and interactive sessions to allow community members to experience the practical aspects of emerging technologies.
*   Further, provide easily accessible learning resources that dive deep into the nuances of technology and its use cases, such as online courses and tutorials, to empower community members with the knowledge to understand and utilize these technologies better.
*   Social-media platforms like X, Reddit, and Telegram can be utilized to hold online forums where community members can discuss concerns, ideas, and potential applications of these technologies, fostering inclusive conversations with unique perspectives. It goes without saying that such sessions can be held offline too.
*   We can choose to create a community ambassador program where individuals can be elected to act as representatives who bridge the gap between us, the technology developers, and the community, facilitating better communication and increased engagement.
*   Look to showcase localized use cases and success stories of how the particular technologies we are working with have positively impacted communities already, making the benefits more tangible to our community members.
*   Remember to establish channels for community feedback on our ongoing projects and keep up with said feedback continuously, ensuring that the development of upcoming technologies aligns with community values and needs.
*   Community members can also be made part of workshops on the ethical considerations and responsible use of AI, blockchain, and robotics, and other tech we may be working with to instill a sense of responsibility among them.
*   Host community meetups on a regular basis, providing networking opportunities for tech enthusiasts, professionals, and all community members in general to connect with each other and collaborate.
*   We can also initiate hackathons and other innovation challenges that invite community participation and promise exciting rewards, thus encouraging collaborative problem-solving for the project’s future, along with creativity and innovation.

Increasing community engagement for emerging technology projects overall involves creating an environment of collaboration and learning, along with instilling a sense of shared ownership among community members. Open-source communities are a great way to promote innovation, collaboration, and share ideas and solutions for the developing technologies. A diverse community of developers, leaders, and consumers can come together to address the needs and challenges of the technologies. By actively creating such inclusive environment, we can look for new ways to empower and appreciate community members, while our projects stand to gain valuable insights, along with widespread support for successful implementation.

[Ensuring ethical decision-making as developers](#contents.xhtml#sc3_330a)

Now, our responsibility as a developer involved in the emerging technologies space is multifaceted, covering quite a few heavy aspects. One of these factors is to ensure transparency, take accountability, and essentially put humans first always. How to ensure we and the technology we build remain ethical at all times? Here are some solutions:

*   Implement ethics training programs for team members involved in development and more, keeping them informed about evolving ethical considerations in blockchain, AI, robotics, automation, or other areas of futuristic technologies we may be working on.
*   Aside from specific local and global regulatory guidelines that may be imposed in the times ahead, develop and integrate our own ethical frameworks and guidelines into the technology development process, ensuring ethical considerations are part of the project from inception to deployment.
*   Form ethics committees within development teams ensuring participation from a diverse group and incorporate perspectives from various backgrounds to ensure a comprehensive ethical evaluation of our projects.
*   Define clear accountability mechanisms within development teams, ensuring that individuals know to take responsibility for the ethical implications of their contributions.
*   Help developers learn from ethical considerations and any footfalls in the area in previous projects, promoting a culture of continuous improvement.
*   Ensuring ethical decision-making is an ongoing process. Therefore, regularly conduct assessments to evaluate the potential ethical impact of technologies on various stakeholders, including the end-users and communities.
*   Foster transparent communication within development teams and with the entire community, ensuring that ethical decisions are openly discussed, and new ideas are shared, so as to address potential concerns collectively.
*   We can also implement anonymous reporting channels to encourage team members to voice their ethical concerns without fear of retaliation.
*   Make sure to stay informed about relevant laws and regulations pertaining to technological development in our particular sector, ensuring compliance with legal and ethical standards as our project progresses.
*   When it comes to AI in particular, adhere to established ethical AI design principles, such as fairness, transparency, accountability, and inclusivity, in the development of AI systems. It is important to transparently declare the data used for training, for instance, and to ensure anonymity in case of any potentially sensitive information being used.
*   Another good way to ensure we consider the ethical viewpoint continuously is to assess the potential impact of upcoming technologies on society, considering both intended and unintended consequences, to anticipate and address ethical challenges the future may bring.
*   Last but not least, the most obvious step adopts a user-centric design approach, prioritizing the needs, safety, and privacy of end-users to align technology development with ethical principles.

By integrating ethics in technology development, we as a project lead or developer can foster a culture of responsibility. This is one of the most careful considerations to fulfill the true purpose of all technological advancements, which is to enhance human lives. Ethical decision-making will set us on the right path to make sure the technology serves humans, and not the other way around.

The United Nations Educational, Scientific and Cultural Organization (UNESCO, 2024) Global Forum on Ethics of AI highlights the important guidelines, and which aims to ensure ethical AI governance across global industries. UNESCO emphasizes principles of fairness, inclusivity, sustainability, and non-discrimination policies. It also promoted the use of Ethical Impact Assessment tools to evaluate and understand the impact of AI on society. The Global Forum further introduced Global AI Ethics Observatory to monitor and analyze ethical practices globally. UNESCO has already conducted a comprehensive diagnostic of AI in nearly fifty countries, which provided insights into its capabilities and challenges. These initiatives and frameworks promote a collaborative ecosystem to build better converging technologies ahead source: [https://www.unesco.org/en/forum-ethics-ai](https://www.unesco.org/en/forum-ethics-ai).

[Adopting sustainable practices for a better tomorrow](#contents.xhtml#sc3_331a)

Finally, there will be no tomorrow if we do not think of minimizing the environmental impact of all the upcoming technologies, even with all the advancements we have been making with said tech. The solution lies in renewable energy usage, and more. Let us take a look at some sustainable practices:

*   Develop and use energy-efficient algorithms in blockchain, AI, and robotics to minimize resource consumption and promote sustainability. For example, blockchains can switch to consensus mechanisms that consume less energy, such as proof of stake.
*   Embrace renewable energy sources for powering blockchain nodes, AI data centers, AI servers, and robotic systems, reducing the carbon footprint associated with technology infrastructure.
*   Apply circular economy principles in hardware manufacturing, promoting the reuse and recycling of components to reduce electronic waste.
*   Adopt a minimalistic design approach in software and hardware development, focusing on essential functionalities to reduce unnecessary resource consumption.
*   Introduce efficient manufacturing processes for robotics and automation systems, minimizing waste during production and assembly.
*   Implement tools and methodologies to measure and track the carbon footprint of blockchain networks, AI models, and automated systems, fostering accountability.
*   Invest in eco-friendly AI data center designs that prioritize energy efficiency, and the use of sustainable materials.
*   Integrate biodegradable and eco-friendly materials in the manufacturing of robotic components, contributing to sustainable end-of-life disposal.
*   Ensure sustainability in the supply chain by partnering with suppliers committed to ethical and environmentally responsible practices.
*   AI-driven monitoring systems can be used to assess and mitigate the environmental impact of technology operations in real-time.
*   Promote remote work practices facilitated by AI and automation-driven collaboration tools, reducing the need for daily commuting and lowering associated environmental impacts.
*   Engage in public awareness campaigns to educate users, developers, and businesses about the importance of adopting sustainable practices in the tech industry.
*   The synergy of AI and blockchain helps in developing innovative tools for climate analysis and actions such as tracking carbon emissions, predicting optimized solutions, etc. It can also help optimize supply chains for sustainable agricultural practices, renewable energy distribution, and waste management.
*   With the help of AI, we can also streamline blockchain operations, monitor energy consumption, deploy market-driven solutions, and implement sustainable initiatives at the root-level.

By integrating sustainable practices into the development, deployment, and usage of blockchain, AI, robotics, automation, and more, the proponents in emerging technology sectors can play a pivotal role in building a more environmentally conscious and resilient future. Keep innovating to come up with more ways to ensure sustainability remains a priority for us throughout our project.

Now, we know how to solve the most potent challenges keeping futuristic technologies out of reach of the common man and build a technologically enriched tomorrow.

Before we head into the very final chapter with a recap of everything we have discussed in this book, let us take a quick look at some issues of green energy that need addressing for better adaptability too.

[Challenges and solutions: Green energy](#contents.xhtml#sc2_332a)

The technologies of tomorrow require a hundredfold more energy than what humankind has consumed historically. While this may seem like a small price to pay for the degree of convenience, efficiency, and innovation they can give us, our world cannot afford to spend the remainder of our conventional energy resources behind them, as it becomes a question of survival for our kind. This is why green energy is a must to see the future we envisioned at the beginning of this chapter. Some key innovation trends include, agrivoltaics and floatovoltaics in agriculture, luminescent solar concentrators, power consumption prediction in big data analysis, decentralized hydro-power generation, biofuel production, AI-based renewable energy automation, and more.

However, so far renewable energy remains out of reach for the common man due to high costs and low availability. Let us take a closer look at the issues that keep green energy from going mainstream and try to find solutions to those.

[Challenges in green energy](#contents.xhtml#sc3_333a)

Here are the pressing issues to be solved:

*   Green energy sources such as solar and wind are intermittent, leading to challenges in maintaining a reliable and consistent energy supply.
*   The upfront costs of installing green energy systems, such as solar panels or wind turbines, can be a barrier to adoption, despite long-term cost savings.
*   Transitioning existing energy infrastructure to accommodate green energy requires significant investments in grid upgrades and smart technologies for efficient distribution.
*   Compatibility issues with existing infrastructure and legacy systems can slow down the integration of green energy solutions into the broader energy grid.
*   Developing efficient and cost-effective energy storage technologies is crucial to store excess energy generated during peak times for use during low production periods.
*   The availability of materials for energy storage technologies, such as lithium-ion batteries, may face limitations, requiring exploration of alternative and sustainable materials.
*   Lack of awareness and misconceptions about green energy can hinder widespread acceptance, emphasizing the need for education and awareness campaigns.
*   Large-scale deployment of renewable energy infrastructure may require significant land use, potentially impacting ecosystems and local environments.
*   For companies, the shift to green energy may require retraining the existing workforce and developing new skill sets, leading to potential challenges in the job market.
*   Designing green energy systems to withstand extreme weather events and other climate-related challenges is crucial for their long-term effectiveness.
*   Continued research and development are essential to enhance efficiency and reduce the costs of green energy technologies for broader adoption.
*   On the part of regulatory bodies around the world, establishing supportive policies, incentives, and regulations is crucial to encourage businesses and individuals to invest in and adopt green energy solutions.
*   Ensuring equitable access to green energy solutions globally is a challenge, with disparities in resource availability and economic conditions among different regions.
*   Addressing climate change and promoting green energy necessitates global cooperation and agreements to facilitate the sharing of knowledge, technology, and resources.

This goes without saying, but addressing all these challenges requires collaborative efforts from governments, industries, and communities to create a sustainable and resilient future powered by green energy.

[Effective solutions](#contents.xhtml#sc3_334a)

Here are some ways the aforementioned issues can be addressed:

*   Those involved in green energy production can choose to invest in advanced energy storage technologies like batteries and pumped hydro storage to store any excess energy during peak production for later use.
*   Existing infrastructure can be upgraded with smart grid technologies to efficiently distribute and manage green energy, improving grid reliability.
*   Proponents in the sector can also conduct awareness campaigns to educate the public about the benefits of green energy and dispel misconceptions.
*   Project heads can implement strategic planning and environmental impact assessments to lower the carbon footprint of renewable energy projects.
*   Companies directly involved in the creation or usage of green energy must adopt training and educational programs to aid the workforce with the skills needed for the green energy sector, ensuring a smooth transition.
*   A phased approach can be taken to integrate green energy infrastructure with existing systems, allowing for gradual compatibility.
*   Climate-resilient designs in green energy infrastructure can be brought on to withstand natural events and ensure continuous operation.
*   Explorations and investments in alternative materials for energy storage to reduce dependence on scarce resources are necessary.
*   Blockchain can magnify traceability and transparency in green energy transactions, bringing in peer to peer trading of energy, leading to better grid management.
*   Smart contracts and decentralized protocols can further provide secure energy trades, automated for the convenience of customers. This would allow for better accessibility as well.
*   Predictive analytics in green energy installations can optimize performance with predictive maintenance. Further, real-time monitoring with AI tools allows early detection of any potential problems, allowing for prompt action and optimized generation of energy.
*   Financial incentives, tax credits, and subsidies can be brought on to make the initial investment in green energy more attractive for both individuals and businesses.
*   Governments can allocate funding and resources for research and development in the green energy sector, encouraging a collaboration between public and private sectors to drive technological advancements, and increasing public faith in these technologies in the process.
*   Further, regulatory bodies can bring out clear and supportive policies, regulations, and incentives at local and national levels to encourage the adoption of green energy solutions.
*   International collaboration and agreements will be required to facilitate the exchange of knowledge, technology, and resources for global green energy initiatives.
*   Global cooperation must be fostered to address energy disparities, with more developed nations providing support and resources to enable access to green energy in developing regions.

By implementing these solutions, stakeholders can overcome challenges and accelerate the widespread adoption of green energy, contributing to a more sustainable and environmentally friendly energy landscape. AI helps in optimizing energy grids which helps enhance energy efficiency and sustainability. According to the International Energy Agency’s Electricity Market Report 2023, renewable energy resources are expected to satisfy over 90% of growth by 2025. The use of smart grids, energy-saving appliances, and sustainability practices can increase the use of renewable resources and also reduce the non-renewable energy consumption. Smart grid energy applications have seen quite a rise in the market, over the years, promising a better outlook for energy consumption. However, the most important factors to bring about this change would be continuous innovation on the part of those involved in relevant projects, and favorable policies on the part of world leaders. The cost of green energy has been dropping rapidly—solar cells are now more than 90% cheaper than less than two decades ago. Many markets have already had energy costs drop temporarily to zero when they produced excess energy thanks to green sources adding to their output.

[Conclusion](#contents.xhtml#sc2_335a)

With that, we have reached the end of our chapter. This time we learned all about the most pressing issues clouding the path of futuristic technologies like AI, blockchain, robotics, automation, and more, and tried to think of some ways they can build our tomorrow seamlessly, free of these challenges. Then we moved on to talking about green energy—what challenges remain to bring it to the mainstream, and how exactly those can be solved.

While developers and other stakeholders in emerging technology sectors deal with the issues we discussed, and more problems to arise in the future, it remains true that these innovative technologies are indomitable. The challenges of today will pave the way for a more efficient and convenient future tomorrow.

The convergence of advanced technologies like, AI, blockchain, robotics, IoT, 5G, etc., will drive tremendous innovation and growth worldwide. The challenges arising due to such advancements can be tackled with diverse expertise, educational resources, and ethical and sustainable approaches which align with societal values and integrity.

In the next and final chapter of this book, we go through everything we have discussed in this book in a quick recap. We hope this journey has been informative and enjoyable for us!

[Questions](#contents.xhtml#sc2_336a)

1.  What are the most pressing issues the technologies of tomorrow face, keeping them from going mainstream?
    
    Answer: Some issues include a lack of community engagement, creating inequalities and rendering workers jobless, and of course, a lack of ethical decision-making.
    
2.  How can developers empower individuals to adopt futuristic technologies?
    
    Answer: Implementing easily accessible educational programs surrounding the technologies of tomorrow is a good starting point.
    
3.  What are the common regulatory issues with blockchain?
    
    Answer: Non-compliance with KYC policies and anti-money laundering acts are the pressing concerns with blockchain regulations.
    
4.  How can community members be provided with a greater level of engagement in the creation of upcoming projects in the emerging technologies sector?
    
    Answer: Supporting and creating collaborative learning platforms where users can share experiences, insights, and best practices related to emerging technologies can help with fostering this engagement.
    
5.  Which measures to adopt to ensure ethical decision-making for team leads?
    
    Answer: Widespread education as well as a set of well-defined guidelines can help out team leads.
    
6.  Why AI needs to be regulated?
    
    Answer: The risk of misinformation spread can have adverse impact on the society. The autonomous nature of the AI may lead to disastrous damage, without any stringent regulations.
    
7.  Why should we promote the use of green technology?
    
    Answer: It is crucial to promote sustainability, minimize environmental damage, and develop energy-efficient solutions through innovation.
    
8.  What are some sustainable practices projects utilizing the technologies of tomorrow can adopt? Why are they necessary?
    
    Answer: One would be the conscious usage of green energy, which can help a project stay sustainable in the long-term and appeal to users.
    
9.  What challenges keep green energy from reaching the common man?
    
    Answer: High costs and lower availability are two challenges to solve.
    
10.  What are the solutions for these challenges?
     
     Answer: Financial incentives and tax credits can be used to empower users and increase the adoption of green energy.
     
11.  How do we regulate advanced technologies like, AI and robotics?
     
     Answer: For starters, authorities must implement mandatory testing, hold tech developers accountable and classify AI into risk tiers based on their advancements and capabilities.
     

# [CHAPTER 13The Future of Interconnected Technologies](#contents.xhtml#ch13a)

[Introduction](#contents.xhtml#sc2_337a)

Now, as we reach the end of our journey together in this book, this chapter explores the insights of decentralization and the historical evolution, current scenario, and the future of the most prominent technologies of tomorrow—AI, blockchain, robotics, automation, and green energy. We explored the use cases and pressing issues and solutions for these technologies, as well as their potential and sustainability. We also talked about the challenges to the growth of these technologies and discussed solutions. As we adapt to these rapid technological advancements, we will also discuss how the future looks with the convergence of AI, blockchain, automation, and robotics, alongside other emerging futuristic technologies and green energy.

Our overall objective in this book has been to highlight the benefits and scope of the technologies of tomorrow so everyone from the common man to enthusiasts looking to enter the sectors has a good idea of what they are getting into and what the future holds. On that note, we do hope we have achieved this objective and made this book a knowledgeable and exciting ride.

Now, we will briefly review what we have learned in this book to refresh our memory.

[Structure](#contents.xhtml#sc2_338a)

This chapter covers the following topics:

*   Decentralization and its aspects
*   Technologies of tomorrow
*   Convergence of blockchain, AI, robotics and automation
*   Green energy for emerging technologies
*   Embracing the future with emerging technologies
*   Distant future of AI, robotics, and blockchain
*   Additional futuristic technologies

[Objectives](#contents.xhtml#sc2_339a)

Our goal is to remind the readers of all the core takeaways from this book: from decentralization of power in the coming times to futuristic technologies like blockchain, artificial intelligence, robotics, and automation and their different features them, to the potential of the convergence of such technologies. Finally, we also discuss the role of each one of us in making this future we visualize into a reality.

To add a little fun to all the learning we have been doing, next, we set out to imagine what the world might look like 20, 30, or 50 years from now, guided by these technologies of tomorrow.

[Decentralization and its aspects](#contents.xhtml#sc2_340a)

At the very beginning of this book, our objective was to understand decentralization. We learned about the operations of decentralized organizations, the components and benefits of a decentralized system, the history of decentralization, and of course—blockchain and, in turn, DeFi—the two most advanced frontiers of decentralized technology today.

[Decentralization and decentralized technologies](#contents.xhtml#sc3_341a)

Decentralization refers to the distribution of power, authority, or control across multiple entities rather than being concentrated in a single central authority—whether in a governance structure, in a financial system, or elsewhere. In various contexts, decentralization may involve the dispersal of decision-making, resources, or governance.

Decentralized technologies like blockchain are systems or platforms that operate without a central authority or intermediary. These technologies make use of distributed networks of participants and peer-to-peer architectures to achieve consensus and enable expected functionalities without relying on a single controlling entity.

[History of decentralization](#contents.xhtml#sc3_342a)

The concept of decentralization in computing emerged with the development of early computer networks like ARPANET. These networks worked to decentralize information exchange by allowing multiple computers to communicate. ARPANET was, of course, the earliest known predecessor to the internet we know today.

The Internet as we know it today can be divided into Web 1.0, Web 2.0, and Web 3.0. The first one is static, the second one is much more accessible but increasingly centralized, and the third focuses on decentralization and security of user information, along with more immersive user interfaces.

The rise of peer-to-peer (P2P) file-sharing systems like BitTorrent challenged centralized models by enabling direct sharing between users without a central server.

Bitcoin was introduced in 2009 by Satoshi Nakamoto, which brought about a completely new frontier of decentralization, the most popular to date, in fact. Blockchain, the underlying technology, allows decentralized and trustless transactions, eliminating the need for central authorities like banks.

Ethereum then expanded the possibilities of blockchain by introducing smart contracts, which are self-fulfilling agreements with the terms agreed upon by both parties directly written into code. This further led to decentralized applications (dApps) beyond simple financial transactions with cryptocurrencies.

Even as Bitcoin introduced the concept of DeFi to the world after the global financial crisis of 2008, Ethereum was the blockchain that started exploring the entire scope of DeFi. The rise of DeFi was marked by blockchain-based financial services, such as lending, borrowing, and trading, demonstrating the potential to decentralize traditional financial activities.

Then came decentralized autonomous organizations (DAOs), which are enterprises represented by rules put into a transparent computer program that is controlled by all organization members and not influenced by any centralized structure.

As mentioned before, the concept of Web 3.0 envisions a decentralized web where users have more control over their data and interactions. Projects aim to create decentralized alternatives to traditional web services, and blockchain, along with AI, will be one of the technologies most used in this version of the World Wide Web. This is one of the most exciting applications of decentralized technologies to look forward to.

Now, numerous blockchain platforms and cryptocurrencies continue to explore and implement decentralized applications, smart contracts, and governance mechanisms, contributing to the evolution of decentralized technologies. DeFi has emerged as one of the spaces close to achieving mainstream recognition.

[Decentralized finance use cases](#contents.xhtml#sc4_343a)

The most popular use cases of DeFi till date are as follows:

*   Lending and borrowing: This is where users can lend out their crypto holdings to earn interest, while borrowers can deposit some collateral and immediately take out loans without waiting for long approval processes or extensive paperwork.
*   Decentralized exchanges (DEXs): These platforms allow us to trade in digital assets in a peer-to-peer manner, again with no centralized authorities involved.
*   Stablecoins: These are one type of crypto pegged to physical or digital assets for stable valuation.
*   Tokenization: DeFi platforms can allow us to tokenize real-world assets for easy trades.
*   Staking: We can consider this one form of DeFi lending. Essentially, we lock up our crypto on native blockchains/pools for a minimum amount of time to earn interest and other blockchain rewards.

Overall, the history and present of decentralization and relevant technologies reflect a continuous evolution driven by steady advancements and a desire for more inclusive, transparent, and resilient systems. Ongoing developments in blockchain, cryptocurrency, and just decentralized technologies in general suggest a growing shift toward decentralized models across various sectors, as there is growing demand.

[Technologies of tomorrow](#contents.xhtml#sc2_344a)

We explored the technologies this book would discuss, even though we had already learned a little about blockchain in [Chapter 1](#ch01.xhtml), The Foundations of Decentralization. The four key technologies which were at the center of the discussion will be discussed in the upcoming sections.

[Artificial intelligence](#contents.xhtml#sc3_345a)

Artificial intelligence (AI) involves the creation of computer systems that can be programmed to perform tasks that otherwise require human intelligence, such as learning, reasoning, problem-solving, and understanding natural language. ML, NLP, computer vision, speech recognition, and autonomous systems are all part of AI.

[Types of AI](#contents.xhtml#sc4_346a)

As we learned, AI is broadly categorized into two main types based on its capabilities, such as:

*   Narrow AI or weak AI: Narrow AI is made to perform one specific task or a narrow range of tasks. Therefore, it essentially excels in a predefined domain but lacks the ability to employ its sector-specific intelligence in different areas. The examples here include virtual personal assistants like Siri and Alexa, image recognition systems, language translation apps, and more.
*   General AI or strong AI: This one refers to a form of intelligence that can comprehend, learn, and apply knowledge across a broad range of tasks, similar to human intelligence. However, achieving true general AI is a complex and ongoing challenge.

[Key technologies involved in AI](#contents.xhtml#sc4_347a)

AI encompasses quite a large variety of technologies, some of which include:

*   ML is one subset of AI that involves the development of algorithms and models that enable systems to learn from and improve based on experiences (chosen sets of data) without being explicitly programmed.
*   NLP enables systems and machinery to understand, interpret, and generate human language and different manners of speaking. NLP takes care of tasks like language translation, sentiment analysis, and chatbot interactions.
*   Computer vision allows machines to understand and make decisions based on visual data. It can accomplish tasks like image recognition and classification, object detection, and video analysis.
*   Speech recognition technology enables machines to convert spoken language into text, facilitating voice-based interactions. These mechanisms can identify speakers and learn to adopt a customized approach and recommendations for each unique user.
*   Neural networks are there to mimic the structure and functioning of the human brain for AI systems.
*   Deep learning, not to be confused with ML, can bring models that excel at processing data in large amounts or complex data.
*   Reinforcement learning is another AI technique that involves training models to make sequences of decisions by receiving feedback as rewards or penalties. As the name suggested, this mechanism reinforces the correct approach in AI machinery.

There are also expert systems, which are basically AI systems that possess similar decision-making abilities as that of a human expert in a particular sector. To achieve that, these systems use a rule-based approach.

[Blockchain](#contents.xhtml#sc3_348a)

Blockchain is a distributed ledger technology that enables secure, transparent, and tamper-resistant recording of transactions across a worldwide network of computers. Cryptocurrencies like Bitcoin and Ethereum, smart contracts, DeFi, and secure data sharing are some of the most popular applications of blockchain so far. However, the technology has already made its way into a range of non-crypto use cases—from supply chain optimization to healthcare to education to cybersecurity.

[Core components of blockchain](#contents.xhtml#sc4_349a)

The following are the key elements that make up a blockchain:

*   Blocks, which are digital representations of sets of data in a blockchain. Each block contains a certain number of transactions or information and has a unique identifier called a cryptographic hash. The blockchain is formed by linking these blocks together. Each block contains the hash of the previous block, creating a chain of blocks.
*   A blockchain operates on a network of nodes (basically computers that connect participants to the network) distributed globally. Each full node stores a live copy of the entire blockchain, and there is no need for a central authority controlling the network.
*   Consensus mechanisms ensure that all nodes in a blockchain network agree upon the state of the blockchain and contribute to decision-making. This is a core feature that maintains true decentralization of power on a blockchain. Different mechanisms, such as proof of work (PoW) for Bitcoin and proof of stake (PoS) for Ethereum, are used to achieve consensus.
*   Cryptographic hash functions are used to create unique identifiers (hashes) for blocks, as mentioned above. These functions ensure the integrity of data within blocks and the link between blocks, and, of course, keep the blockchain safe from tampering by bad actors.
*   Smart contracts, which execute themselves once all conditions laid out by counterparties in a transaction are signed. By automating contractual agreements, smart contracts render centralized intermediaries unnecessary.
*   The blockchain ledger is immutable, meaning that it cannot be altered or tampered with. This ensures the integrity of the chain's historical record of transactions.
*   Blockchain operates on a P2P network where the nodes communicate directly with each other for decision-making, transaction settlements, and more. This reinforces the decentralized network structure of blockchain and enhances both security and resilience.
*   Public and private key pairs are used for secure digital signatures and for the maximized security of wallets kept by users on the blockchain. Transactions are signed with a private key, while the public key is shared with other users to receive transactions.

[Use cases of blockchain](#contents.xhtml#sc4_350a)

Blockchain has quite a wide range of use cases, both within the realm of cryptocurrencies and beyond. Here are the most prominent ones:

*   The most well-known use of blockchain is in the creation of cryptocurrencies, such as Bitcoin, Ethereum, and others. Blockchain brings a decentralized and secure way to conduct peer-to-peer transactions, allowing cryptocurrencies to present an alternative to traditional finance.
*   DeFi applications that emerge out of the crypto sector can leverage blockchain to provide financial services, such as lending, borrowing, trading, and yield farming, without the need for traditional intermediaries like banks.
*   Beyond crypto, blockchain allows the creation of tokens representing real-world or digital assets. These tokens can be traded on blockchain platforms for better accessibility and exposure and represent ownership and value.
*   Blockchain simplifies and accelerates cross-border payments by eliminating intermediaries and reducing transaction costs as well. It enables faster and more cost-effective international transactions.
*   Blockchain can come in handy for fundraising for a project. It facilitates fundraising through initial coin offerings (ICO) or token sales. Projects can issue their own tokens to raise capital with blockchain, and investors can participate in these offerings with better accessibility due to the online medium.
*   As one of the biggest benefits in the digital age, blockchain offers a secure and decentralized way to manage digital identities. Users can have enhanced control over their personal information, reducing the risk of identity theft.
*   Blockchain can be used to secure the storage and sharing of healthcare data. This way, patients have better control over their own medical records, and healthcare providers can access accurate and up-to-date information through the shared ledger.
*   Blockchain improves transparency and traceability across supply chains. The distributed ledger allows participants (nodes) to track the movement of goods from production to delivery, reducing any errors or frauds and ensuring product authenticity.
*   Blockchain can enhance both the security and transparency of voting systems, something much needed around the world to preserve the integrity of elections. It provides an immutable record of votes, reducing the risk of fraud or errors.
*   Blockchain also streamlines real estate transactions by providing a transparent and tamper-resistant ledger. It reduces the need for intermediaries and enhances the efficiency of property transfers, with tokenization thrown into the mix.
*   Blockchain can be used to timestamp and secure intellectual property, such as patents and copyrights. Again, the immutable record of ownership and origin comes in handy.
*   As we have discussed green energy in this book, blockchain can enable peer-to-peer energy trading by allowing users to buy and sell excess renewable energy directly to each other. With this use case, blockchain supports decentralized and green energy markets.

[Robotics](#contents.xhtml#sc3_351a)

Robotics involves the design, construction, and deployment of robotic machinery to perform a wide variety of tasks autonomously or semi-autonomously. This includes physical robots and software-driven robotic processes. Robotics has many applications in today's world, from industrial automation to healthcare robotics to autonomous vehicles to drones, and there are personal assistants like Amazon’s Alexa as well.

[Types of robots in use today](#contents.xhtml#sc4_352a)

Diverse types of robots are being created today for a variety of applications, namely:

*   Service robots are there to perform different services for humans, and they can be programmed to operate in both commercial and domestic settings. There are robots for cleaning and quick customer service, for example.
*   Autonomous vehicles are powered by robotics and equipped with systems for navigation and control. Self-driving cars and drones make for faster logistics and better road safety.
*   Industrial robots are used in manufacturing and industrial settings for a range of tasks like welding, assembly, painting, and packaging- repetitive or hazardous tasks better left to robots for better accuracy and safety than their human counterparts. These robots are often programmable, and they operate on production lines.
*   Medical robots are designed for, well, medical applications, from surgery to rehabilitation to diagnostics to patient assistance. Robotics-enabled surgeries are slowly becoming quite commonplace, introducing precise and minimally invasive work.
*   Agricultural robots are designed for tasks such as planting, harvesting, and monitoring crops. The aim here is to increase efficiency and reduce manual labor with the help of robotics.
*   There are construction robots for activities like bricklaying, welding, and concrete pouring. Again, the purpose is to improve safety for human workers, along with efficiency in the task.
*   Search and rescue robots can be created and deployed in disaster-stricken areas to assist in search and rescue operations. They can navigate through terrains that are too challenging for human rescuers and locate survivors.
*   Humanoid robots are created to resemble and emulate human behavior, and they are, of course, modeled after real people. These robots are usually tasked with helping humans feel at ease in various situations. For example, social robots can engage in conversations like humans, recognize emotions, and provide companionship. They can be employed to assist the elderly and in healthcare.

[Automation](#contents.xhtml#sc3_353a)

Distinct from robotics, automation refers to the use of technology to perform tasks with very little to no human intervention. It can involve both physical processes and software-driven automation. Automation is historically older than all three of the aforementioned technologies and is currently used for manufacturing automation, home automation, business process automation, and robotic process automation (RPA).

[Automation use cases](#contents.xhtml#sc4_354a)

Automation is more common in our daily lives than we think. Here are the most prominent use cases:

*   Businesses use automation to streamline routine processes like data entry and customer support. This reduces errors and speeds up data-centric operations.
*   On behalf of marketing teams, automation can further be used to schedule and send emails, segment audiences, and even analyze campaign performances. This streamlines email marketing efforts.
*   Customer relationship management (CRM) systems make use of automation to handle customer interactions, track any leads, and also initiate follow-up procedures.
